## 1. Your #41 comment — rigorous negotiation testing ✅
Added **§6.5 "Testing strategy — automatic language negotiation"** to the project doc and replied on the PR. It's a **negotiation matrix N1–N9** where each row is an automated test, with the headline guarantee front-and-center: **a Kannada browser gets Kannada and a Hindi browser gets Hindi, automatically** — plus `?lang` override, persisted-pref-wins, region-subtag normalization (`kn-IN`→`kn`), unsupported→`en` fallback, missing-key→`en`, the static-site shim, and the backend. Each row maps to the slice that proves it (B1 detection now → B5/B6 content → B3 shim → B7 backend), and it's wired into the **DoD as a hard gate** + a manual owner-machine font-render eyeball. **#41 is CI-green — safe to merge.**

## 2. Made it real in B1 (#42) ✅
Added the concrete browser-language detection tests (**N1/N3–N7**) — stubbing `navigator.language` and asserting the negotiation. **84 web tests green** (was 79), typecheck + build clean.

## 3. Flyer in the top nav + own-model page (#44) ✅
- **Top nav:** a **Flyer** entry (between *Own your model* and *Roadmap*) — verified live (screenshot above).

### 337. user (2026-06-21T06:42:33.330Z)

1	// The fast-entry query bar (PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md P3). A deterministic, OFFLINE parser turns
2	// plain text ("top 15 longest", "over 10s") into a selection over the SAME shared set the
3	// checkboxes use. It is honest by construction: a live "parsed-pill" shows exactly how the
4	// text was understood, and concepts with no data behind them (shot-type / player / score) are
5	// greyed out as "not available yet" — never silently faked.
6	import { useMemo, useState } from "react";
7	import type { FormEvent } from "react";
8	import { parseQuery, summarize } from "../lib/query";
9	import type { ParsedQuery } from "../lib/query";
10	
11	interface Props {
12	  /** Apply a parsed query to the rally set (App runs runQuery + writes the shared selection).
13	   * `source` distinguishes a typed-and-submitted query from an example-chip tap (for the trace). */
14	  onApply: (q: ParsedQuery, source: "typed" | "example") => void;
15	}
16	
17	// Each example is a real, parseable query — tapping one fills the box AND applies it. Led by rally
18	// LENGTH (the trusted axis); shot-count filtering exists but is experimental, so it's not featured
19	// here (a test pins that every example parses to a recognized, fully-available query).
20	export const EXAMPLES = ["over 10s", "top 15 longest", "shortest first", "first 10"];
21	
22	const CHIP = "rounded-full px-2.5 py-0.5 text-xs font-medium";
23	
24	export function QueryBar({ onApply }: Props) {
25	  const [value, setValue] = useState("");
26	  const parsed = useMemo(() => parseQuery(value), [value]);
27	  const clauses = summarize(parsed);
28	  const typed = value.trim().length > 0;
29	
30	  const applyText = (text: string) => {
31	    setValue(text);
32	    onApply(parseQuery(text), "example");
33	  };
34	  const submit = (e: FormEvent) => {
35	    e.preventDefault();
36	    if (parsed.recognized) onApply(parsed, "typed");
37	  };
38	
39	  return (
40	    <div>
41	      <form onSubmit={submit} className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2">
42	        <input
43	          type="text"
44	          value={value}
45	          onChange={(e) => setValue(e.target.value)}
46	          placeholder="e.g. over 10s · top 15 longest · shortest first"
47	          aria-label="Filter rallies by length, count or order"
48	          className="min-w-[16rem] flex-1 rounded-xl border border-black/15 bg-white px-4 py-2 text-sm text-ink outline-none focus:border-green"
49	        />
50	        <button
51	          type="submit"
52	          disabled={!parsed.recognized}
53	          className="rounded-xl bg-ink px-4 py-2 text-sm font-semibold text-white disabled:opacity-40"
54	        >
55	          Apply
56	        </button>
57	      </form>
58	
59	      {/* Live interpretation — transparent + hand-correctable (no hidden NL guessing). */}
60	      <div aria-live="polite" data-testid="query-pill" className="mt-2 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-1.5">
61	        {clauses.map((c) => (
62	          <span key={c} className={CHIP + " bg-green/15 text-green"}>
63	            {c}
64	          </span>
65	        ))}
66	        {parsed.unavailable.map((u) => (
67	          <span
68	            key={u.token}
69	            title={`"${u.token}" needs ${u.kind} detection — not available yet`}
70	            className={CHIP + " bg-black/5 text-ink/45"}
71	          >
72	            <span className="line-through">{u.token}</span> · not available yet
73	          </span>
74	        ))}
75	        {typed && !parsed.recognized && parsed.unavailable.length === 0 && (
76	          <span className="text-xs text-ink/50">Couldn't read that — try “over 10s” or “top 15 longest”.</span>
77	        )}
78	        {!typed && <span className="text-xs text-ink/50">Type a quick filter, or tap an example.</span>}
79	      </div>
80	
81	      <div className="mt-3 flex flex-wrap gap-2 text-sm">
82	        <span className="text-ink/40">Try:</span>
83	        {EXAMPLES.map((ex) => (
84	          <button
85	            key={ex}
86	            type="button"
87	            onClick={() => applyText(ex)}
88	            className="rounded-full border border-black/15 px-3 py-1 text-xs hover:border-green"
89	          >
90	            {ex}
91	          </button>
92	        ))}
93	      </div>
94	
95	      <p className="mt-2 text-xs text-ink/40">
96	        Filtering by <strong className="text-ink/60">rally length</strong> is fully supported. Shot-count is{" "}
97	        <span className="text-amber-700">experimental</span> (under development).
98	      </p>
99	    </div>
100	  );
101	}
102

### 338. user (2026-06-21T06:42:33.593Z)

1	// The selectable rally grid — one card per play_segment, HONEST fields only:
2	// ordinal (by start order), mm:ss start, and rally LENGTH (the trusted axis). A shot-count chip
3	// is shown ONLY when present, and clearly flagged EXPERIMENTAL — shot detection is still under
4	// development and not yet reliable, so length is what we lead with (owner 2026-06-20).
5	// Never renders server/receiver/events (motion-only fabrications, PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md §4).
6	import type { RallySegment } from "../api/types";
7	import { mmss } from "../lib/format";
8	
9	interface Props {
10	  rallies: RallySegment[];
11	  isSelected: (id: number) => boolean;
12	  onToggle: (id: number) => void;
13	  /** Stable chronological label for a rally (by start_time), so re-sorting via a query
14	   * doesn't renumber the cards. Falls back to display position when absent. */
15	  ordinalOf?: (id: number) => number;
16	}
17	
18	export function RallyGrid({ rallies, isSelected, onToggle, ordinalOf }: Props) {
19	  if (rallies.length === 0) {
20	    return <p className="text-ink/60 text-sm">No rallies to show yet.</p>;
21	  }
22	  return (
23	    <ul className="grid grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(150px,1fr))] gap-3 list-none p-0 m-0">
24	      {rallies.map((r, i) => {
25	        const selected = isSelected(r.id);
26	        const n = ordinalOf ? ordinalOf(r.id) : i + 1;
27	        const shot = r.metadata?.shot_count;
28	        const conf = r.metadata?.shot_count_confidence;
29	        return (
30	          <li key={r.id}>
31	            <button
32	              type="button"
33	              onClick={() => onToggle(r.id)}
34	              aria-pressed={selected}
35	              aria-label={`Rally ${n}${selected ? ", in reel" : ", not in reel"}`}
36	              className={
37	                "w-full text-left rounded-xl border p-3 transition " +
38	                (selected
39	                  ? "border-green bg-green/10"
40	                  : "border-black/10 bg-white hover:border-green/60")
41	              }
42	            >
43	              <div className="flex items-center justify-between">
44	                <span className="font-semibold text-ink">Rally {n}</span>
45	                <span
46	                  aria-hidden="true"
47	                  className={
48	                    "inline-flex h-5 w-5 items-center justify-center rounded-full text-xs " +
49	                    (selected ? "bg-green text-[#04140D]" : "border border-black/20 text-transparent")
50	                  }
51	                >
52	                  ✓
53	                </span>
54	              </div>
55	              <div className="mt-1 text-sm text-ink/70">
56	                {mmss(r.start_time)} <span className="text-ink/40">start</span> · <strong className="text-ink/90">{mmss(r.duration)}</strong> <span className="text-ink/40">long</span>
57	              </div>
58	              {typeof shot === "number" && (
59	                <div
60	                  title={`Shot count is experimental (under development) and may be inaccurate${conf && conf !== "Unknown" ? ` — confidence: ${conf}` : ""}. Filter by rally length instead.`}
61	                  className="mt-2 inline-block rounded-full bg-amber-50 px-2 py-0.5 text-xs text-amber-700"
62	                >
63	                  ~{shot} shots · experimental
64	                </div>
65	              )}
66	            </button>
67	          </li>
68	        );
69	      })}
70	    </ul>
71	  );
72	}

### 339. user (2026-06-21T06:42:40.520Z)

1	// Sticky compile footer (PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md P4): live selection summary, a reel-name input,
2	// the Build button, and — above all — an HONEST min_shot_count warning. The engine silently drops
3	// selected rallies below stitching.min_shot_count; we surface that BEFORE Build so a rally never
4	// vanishes without a reason (D7). We warn, we do NOT override the floor (D11).
5	import { mmss, pluralize } from "../lib/format";
6	import type { FloorRisk } from "../lib/floor";
7	
8	interface Props {
9	  count: number;
10	  totalSec: number;
11	  reelName: string;
12	  onReelNameChange: (v: string) => void;
13	  onBuild: () => void;
14	  /** A compile is in flight (Build wired in P5). */
15	  building?: boolean;
16	  /** Non-null when some selected rallies sit below the engine floor. */
17	  warning: FloorRisk | null;
18	}
19	
20	export function SelectionFooter({ count, totalSec, reelName, onReelNameChange, onBuild, building, warning }: Props) {
21	  const buildDisabled = count === 0 || building || warning?.allDropped === true;
22	
23	  return (
24	    <footer className="fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 border-t border-black/10 bg-white/95 backdrop-blur">
25	      {warning && (
26	        <div
27	          role="status"
28	          data-testid="floor-warning"
29	          className={
30	            "border-b px-6 py-2 text-sm " +
31	            (warning.allDropped
32	              ? "border-red-200 bg-red-50 text-red-800"
33	              : "border-amber-200 bg-amber-50 text-amber-900")
34	          }
35	        >
36	          {warning.allDropped ? (
37	            count === 1 ? (
38	              <>
39	                <strong>Your only selected rally</strong> has fewer than {warning.minShotCount} shots — the engine
40	                would skip it, so the build would fail. Pick a longer rally.
41	              </>
42	            ) : (
43	              <>
44	                <strong>All {count} selected rallies</strong> have fewer than {warning.minShotCount} shots — the engine
45	                skips every one, so the build would fail. Pick longer rallies.
46	              </>
47	            )
48	          ) : (
49	            <>
50	              <strong>{pluralize(warning.atRisk.length, "rally", "rallies")}</strong> with fewer than{" "}
51	              {warning.minShotCount} shots will be skipped when building (the engine's{" "}
52	              <code>min_shot_count</code> floor).
53	            </>
54	          )}
55	        </div>
56	      )}
57	
58	      <div className="mx-auto flex max-w-5xl flex-wrap items-center gap-3 px-6 py-3">
59	        <span className="text-sm text-ink/80" data-testid="selection-summary">
60	          <strong className="text-ink">{pluralize(count, "rally", "rallies")}</strong> selected · {mmss(totalSec)} total
61	        </span>
62	
63	        <label className="ml-auto flex items-center gap-2 text-sm text-ink/60">
64	          <span className="hidden sm:inline">Reel name</span>
65	          <input
66	            type="text"
67	            value={reelName}
68	            onChange={(e) => onReelNameChange(e.target.value)}
69	            placeholder="my-highlights"
70	            aria-label="Reel name"
71	            className="w-40 rounded-lg border border-black/15 bg-white px-3 py-1.5 text-ink outline-none focus:border-green"
72	          />
73	        </label>
74	
75	        <button
76	          type="button"
77	          onClick={onBuild}
78	          disabled={buildDisabled}
79	          className="rounded-full bg-green px-5 py-2 text-sm font-semibold text-[#04140D] disabled:opacity-40"
80	        >
81	          {building ? "Building…" : `Build reel (${count})`}
82	        </button>
83	      </div>
84	    </footer>
85	  );
86	}

### 340. user (2026-06-21T06:42:40.801Z)

1	// The compiled-reel result (PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md P5): once Build → POST /api/compile succeeds,
2	// preview the reel inline (<video> over GET /api/highlights/...) and offer a download. The engine
3	// returns a server-local filepath, NOT a URL (main.py:394), so the SPA builds the URL itself. On a
4	// failure (e.g. the min_shot_count 400) we show the engine's reason — loud, never swallowed.
5	
6	export type BuildState =
7	  | { status: "idle" }
8	  | { status: "building" }
9	  | { status: "done"; url: string; filename: string }
10	  | { status: "error"; error: string };
11	
12	interface Props {
13	  build: BuildState;
14	  onDismiss: () => void;
15	}
16	
17	export function CompileResult({ build, onDismiss }: Props) {
18	  if (build.status === "idle" || build.status === "building") return null;
19	
20	  return (
21	    <section
22	      data-testid="compile-result"
23	      className={
24	        "mt-6 rounded-xl border p-4 " +
25	        (build.status === "error" ? "border-red-200 bg-red-50" : "border-green/30 bg-green/5")
26	      }
27	    >
28	      <div className="flex items-start justify-between gap-3">
29	        <h2 className="font-semibold text-ink">
30	          {build.status === "done" ? "Your reel is ready" : "Build failed"}
31	        </h2>
32	        <button
33	          type="button"
34	          onClick={onDismiss}
35	          aria-label="Dismiss"
36	          className="rounded-full px-2 text-ink/50 hover:text-ink"
37	        >
38	          ✕
39	        </button>
40	      </div>
41	
42	      {build.status === "done" ? (
43	        <div className="mt-3">
44	          {/* eslint-disable-next-line jsx-a11y/media-has-caption — a stitched rally reel has no captions */}
45	          <video
46	            data-testid="reel-video"
47	            src={build.url}
48	            controls
49	            playsInline
50	            className="w-full max-w-xl rounded-lg bg-black"
51	          />
52	          <a
53	            href={build.url}
54	            download={build.filename}
55	            className="mt-3 inline-block rounded-full bg-green px-4 py-2 text-sm font-semibold text-[#04140D]"
56	          >
57	            Download {build.filename}
58	          </a>
59	        </div>
60	      ) : (
61	        <p className="mt-2 text-sm text-red-800" data-testid="compile-error">
62	          {build.error}
63	        </p>
64	      )}
65	    </section>
66	  );
67	}
68

### 391. assistant (2026-06-21T06:52:17.186Z)

// CI guardrails that keep i18n "by design" (FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md §4):
//   1. KEY-PARITY — every locale catalog has EXACTLY the `en` key set (the extensibility proof:
//      drop a stub locale and this lists every gap). Trivially green while only `en` exists; it
//      starts enforcing the moment a kn/hi catalog lands (B5/B6).
//   2. NO-BARE-STRINGS (attribute level, v1) — user-facing component attributes (placeholder /
//      aria-label / title) must route through t(), never a hardcoded literal. A curated start per
//      the doc; an AST-level sweep of JSX text can extend it later.
import { describe, it, expect } from "vitest";
import { readdirSync, readFileSync } from "node:fs";
import { join } from "node:path";

const LOCALES_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "locales");
const SRC_DIR = join(import.meta.dirname, "..");

function flatten(obj: Record<string, unknown>, prefix = ""): string[] {
  const keys: string[] = [];
  for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) {
    const key = prefix ? `${prefix}.${k}` : k;
    if (v && typeof v === "object" && !Array.isArray(v)) keys.push(...flatten(v as Record<string, unknown>, key));
    else keys.push(key);
  }
  return keys.sort();
}

const loadKeys = (locale: string): string[] =>
  flatten(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(LOCALES_DIR, locale, "common.json"), "utf8")) as Record<string, unknown>);

describe("i18n key-parity — every locale has EXACTLY the en keys", () => {
  const enKeys = loadKeys("en");
  const others = readdirSync(LOCALES_DIR, { withFileTypes: true })
    .filter((d) => d.isDirectory() && d.name !== "en")
    .map((d) => d.name);

  it("en is the non-empty source catalog", () => {
    expect(enKeys.length).toBeGreaterThan(20);
  });

  for (const loc of others) {
    it(`${loc} matches en exactly (no missing, no extra keys)`, () => {
      const keys = loadKeys(loc);
      const missing = enKeys.filter((k) => !keys.includes(k));
      const extra = keys.filter((k) => !enKeys.includes(k));
      expect({ locale: loc, missing, extra }).toEqual({ locale: loc, missing: [], extra: [] });
    });
  }
});

describe("no-bare-strings — user-facing component attributes go through t()", () => {
  const FILES = [
    "App.tsx",
    "components/QueryBar.tsx",
    "components/RallyGrid.tsx",
    "components/SelectionFooter.tsx",
    "components/CompileResult.tsx",
    "components/LanguageSwitcher.tsx",
  ];
  // A hardcoded placeholder / aria-label / title with letters is a bare-string regression — it
  // must be an expression ({t(...)}), not a quoted literal.
  const BARE_ATTR = /\b(?:placeholder|aria-label|title)\s*=\s*"[^"]*[A-Za-z]{2}[^"]*"/g;

  for (const f of FILES) {
    it(`${f}: no hardcoded placeholder/aria-label/title`, () => {
      const src = readFileSync(join(SRC_DIR, f), "utf8");
      expect(src.match(BARE_ATTR) ?? []).toEqual([]);
    });
  }
});

1	// @vitest-environment jsdom
2	//
3	// The SPA had NO render tests before this (verified) — this file adds the jsdom safety net for the

5	// missing-locale falls back to English, and ICU plurals format. It mounts the real <App />.
6	import { describe, it, expect, beforeEach, afterEach, vi } from "vitest";
7	import { render, screen, cleanup, fireEvent } from "@testing-library/react";
8	import i18n, { LANG_STORAGE_KEY } from "./index";
9	import App from "../App";
10	
11	beforeEach(async () => {
12	  // No engine in the test env: stub fetch so App's getConfig()/listRallies effects reject quietly
13	  // (App handles this gracefully — cfg stays null) instead of hitting the network.
14	  vi.stubGlobal("fetch", vi.fn(() => Promise.reject(new Error("no engine in test"))));
15	  window.localStorage.clear();
16	  await i18n.changeLanguage("en"); // also guarantees init completed before the first render
17	});
18	
19	afterEach(() => {
20	  cleanup();
21	  vi.unstubAllGlobals();
22	});
23	
24	describe("i18n scaffold (B1)", () => {
25	  it("renders user-facing copy from the en catalog (not raw keys)", () => {
26	    render(<App />);
27	    expect(screen.getByRole("heading", { level: 1 }).textContent).toBe("Pick the rallies for your reel");
28	    expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Select all" })).toBeTruthy();
29	  });
30	
31	  it("B2: the extracted component strings render (no leftover raw keys across the page)", () => {
32	    const { container } = render(<App />);
33	    expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: "Apply" })).toBeTruthy(); // QueryBar
34	    expect(screen.getByRole("button", { name: /Build reel/ })).toBeTruthy(); // SelectionFooter
35	    expect(container.textContent).toContain("rally length"); // <Trans> hero intro + query support
36	    expect(container.textContent).toMatch(/\d+ rallies selected · \d+:\d+ total/); // ICU footer summary
37	    expect(container.textContent).toMatch(/Rally \d+/); // RallyGrid card
38	    // No untranslated catalog keys leaked into the DOM.
39	    expect(container.textContent).not.toMatch(/\b(app|grid|footer|query|mode|result|build)\.[a-zA-Z]+/);
40	  });
41	
42	  it("offers a language switcher and persists the choice (persisted pref wins on next visit)", () => {
43	    render(<App />);
44	    const knBtn = screen.getByRole("button", { name: "ಕನ್ನಡ" });
45	    fireEvent.click(knBtn);
46	    expect(i18n.language).toBe("kn");
47	    expect(window.localStorage.getItem(LANG_STORAGE_KEY)).toBe("kn");
48	    // The switcher reflects the CHOSEN locale even though content falls back to English (no kn
49	    // catalog yet) — resolvedLanguage would wrongly show en here.
50	    expect(knBtn.getAttribute("aria-pressed")).toBe("true");
51	  });
52	
53	  it("falls back to English for a locale with no catalog yet (never a raw key)", async () => {
54	    await i18n.changeLanguage("kn"); // kn catalog ships later (B5) — must still show English now
55	    render(<App />);
56	    expect(screen.getByRole("heading", { level: 1 }).textContent).toBe("Pick the rallies for your reel");
57	  });
58	
59	  it("formats ICU plurals from the catalog", () => {
60	    expect(i18n.t("selection.count", { count: 1 })).toBe("1 rally selected");
61	    expect(i18n.t("selection.count", { count: 3 })).toBe("3 rallies selected");
62	  });
63	});
64	
65	// The owner's bar (#41): a user whose BROWSER/OS language is Kannada/Hindi must auto-get the
66	// respective version. These prove the negotiation NOW at the detection level (B1, en-only); the
67	// kn/hi CONTENT assertions ride the catalogs in B5/B6. See FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL.md §6.5 (N1–N9).
68	describe("browser-language negotiation (B1: N1/N3–N7)", () => {
69	  const detectBases = () => {
70	    const d = i18n.services.languageDetector.detect();
71	    return (Array.isArray(d) ? d : [d]).filter(Boolean).map((l) => String(l).split("-")[0]);
72	  };
73	
74	  it("N1/N5: a Kannada browser (kn-IN) is auto-detected as kn for a first-time visitor", () => {
75	    window.localStorage.clear(); // no persisted preference yet
76	    vi.stubGlobal("navigator", { language: "kn-IN", languages: ["kn-IN", "en-IN"] });
77	    expect(detectBases()).toContain("kn"); // the kn-IN region subtag normalizes to base kn
78	  });
79	
80	  it("N4: a persisted preference beats the browser language (returning user keeps their choice)", () => {
81	    window.localStorage.setItem(LANG_STORAGE_KEY, "kn");
82	    vi.stubGlobal("navigator", { language: "en-US", languages: ["en-US"] });
83	    expect(detectBases()[0]).toBe("kn");
84	  });
85	
86	  it("N3: persisted pref and ?lang are negotiated BEFORE the browser language", () => {
87	    const order = i18n.options.detection?.order ?? [];
88	    expect(order.indexOf("localStorage")).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(0);
89	    expect(order.indexOf("localStorage")).toBeLessThan(order.indexOf("navigator"));
90	    expect(order.indexOf("querystring")).toBeLessThan(order.indexOf("navigator"));
91	  });
92	
93	  it("N6: an unsupported browser language (Tamil) serves English content — never a locale we can't render", async () => {
94	    window.localStorage.clear();
95	    await i18n.changeLanguage("ta"); // ta is not in SUPPORTED_LOCALES

98	  });
99	
100	  it("N7: a key missing from the active locale falls back to English (no raw key)", async () => {
101	    await i18n.changeLanguage("kn"); // kn catalog not shipped yet → every key falls back to en
102	    expect(i18n.t("app.title")).toBe("Pick the rallies for your reel");
103	    expect(i18n.t("bulk.selectAll")).toBe("Select all");
104	  });

### 519. assistant (2026-06-21T07:14:35.682Z)

// KhelSutra Studio i18n screencast: a Kannada-DEFAULT browser auto-renders Kannada,
// then the switcher walks kn -> hi -> en. Proves the owner's success criterion.
import { chromium } from "playwright";

const URL = process.env.URL || "http://localhost:5180";
const KN_TITLE = "ನಿಮ್ಮ ರೀಲ್‌ಗಾಗಿ ರ್ಯಾಲಿಗಳನ್ನು ಆರಿಸಿ";
const HI_TITLE = "अपनी रील के लिए रैलियाँ चुनें";
const EN_TITLE = "Pick the rallies for your reel";
const HOLD = 2600;

const browser = await chromium.launch();
// A Kannada-configured browser: locale sets navigator.language=kn-IN. Fresh context => no stored
// preference => the SPA negotiates from the BROWSER language (the whole point).
const ctx = await browser.newContext({
  locale: "kn-IN",
  viewport: { width: 1280, height: 900 },
  recordVideo: { dir: "video", size: { width: 1280, height: 900 } },
});
const page = await ctx.newPage();
await page.goto(URL, { waitUntil: "domcontentloaded" });

// 1) Kannada renders automatically (browser language → kn).
await page.getByText(KN_TITLE, { exact: true }).waitFor({ timeout: 15000 });
console.log("kn rendered from browser language:", await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.lang));
await page.waitForTimeout(HOLD);
await page.screenshot({ path: "shot-1-kn.png" });

// 2) Switch to Hindi.
await page.locator('button[lang="hi"]').click();
await page.getByText(HI_TITLE, { exact: true }).waitFor({ timeout: 8000 });
console.log("hi rendered:", await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.lang));
await page.waitForTimeout(HOLD);
await page.screenshot({ path: "shot-2-hi.png" });

// 3) Switch to English.
await page.locator('button[lang="en"]').click();
await page.getByText(EN_TITLE, { exact: true }).waitFor({ timeout: 8000 });
console.log("en rendered:", await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.lang));
await page.waitForTimeout(HOLD);
await page.screenshot({ path: "shot-3-en.png" });

await ctx.close(); // flushes the video
await browser.close();
console.log("DONE");

## ✅ The SPA is fully trilingual — proven by the screencast

The screencast (Playwright, `locale: kn-IN`) demonstrates exactly what you asked, and the three frames above are the proof:

| Step | What happens | `<html lang>` |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Kannada-default browser** | The SPA **auto-renders Kannada** — no clicks. The `ಕನ್ನಡ` pill is auto-selected from the browser language. | `kn` |
| **2. Switch to Hindi** | Whole page re-renders in Devanagari | `hi` |
| **3. Switch to English** | Back to English | `en` |

**Artifacts:** `C:\Users\avidu\Projects\khelsutra-screencast\khelsutra-i18n-screencast.mp4` (9s) + per-locale frames (`shot-1-kn.png` / `shot-2-hi.png` / `shot-3-en.png`).

| PR | Slice | |
|---|---|---|

**99 web tests green**, including N1/N2 browser-detection (kn-IN→kn, hi-IN→hi), B5/B6 render assertions, and **key-parity enforcing kn == hi == en exactly**.

## Two honest caveats (important)
1. **The kn/hi translations are AI-drafted by me** (the I18N_PLAN "machine-draft" step). They're solid and consistent with the flyer terminology, but a **native-speaker review** is the right gate before a wide public launch. I flagged this in every catalog PR.
2. **This is the `web/` "Studio" SPA** — the surface that has the language switcher. The **public marketing site** (khelsutra.guru homepage / `/capture`) is **still English-only** — that's **B3** (the shared `t()` shim over the vanilla `site/`), the next slice for full coverage.

113	| N9 | **Backend (engine P2)** | `Accept-Language: kn` to the API | kn customer/report text | B7 (owner-gated) |

115	**Levels & tooling:**
116	- **SPA (`web/`):** jsdom tests stub `navigator.language`/`navigator.languages`, `localStorage`, and the `?lang` query → assert `i18n.language`/`resolvedLanguage` + rendered text per the matrix. *(B1 lands N1/N3–N7 at the **detection** level now — `kn` is supported; the kn/hi **content** assertions ride the catalogs in B5/B6.)*
117	- **Static site (B3):** the shared `t()` shim's negotiation is unit-tested the same way; plus a server test that `Accept-Language: kn`/`?lang=kn` returns the kn-rendered page.
118	- **CI key-parity:** guarantees every locale has every key, so a correctly-negotiated locale can never fall through to a raw key.
119	- **End-to-end (manual, owner machine — per I18N_PLAN §6):** set the real OS/browser to Kannada, then Hindi → load both surfaces → confirm the right language renders **and the Noto fonts display** (the cloud CI container can't render fonts; the owner eyeball is the final gate).

### 597. assistant (2026-06-21T07:31:48.346Z)

All four asks are done — merged, CI-green, no open PRs. Here's the wrap-up.

## 1. ✅ Fonts & assets all local — the tool renders offline (air-gapped)
I audited the Studio SPA and it's **fully self-contained** — verified, then locked it in with a CI guard ([#50](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/50)):
- Noto fonts are **bundled local woff2** (`@fontsource`), `@font-face url()` → local `/assets/*.woff2` (not gstatic).
- `web/index.html` loads **no external `<link>`/`<script>`**; no Google Fonts / CDN anywhere.
- The only `https://` strings in the build are non-loaded text (a React error URL, a Tailwind comment).
- The CI guard fails the build if any external font/CDN reference creeps in.

> One honest note: the **public marketing site** (`khelsutra.guru`) still uses Google Fonts — but that's a hosted/online surface, not the offline appliance tool. Say the word and I'll self-host its fonts too.

## 2. ✅ Screencast stored as proof
- **Bucket:** `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/promo/khelsutra-i18n-screencast.mp4` (uploaded, verified)
- **Local:** `C:\Users\avidu\Projects\khelsutra-screencast\khelsutra-i18n-screencast.mp4` (+ the per-locale frames)

## 3. ✅ Sample-query prompt on the Kannada/Hindi (and English) pages ([#51](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/51))
Every locale now shows users **what kind of queries to type** — verified live in Kannada:
- An instructive hint: *"type `over 10s` for rallies longer than 10 seconds — or tap a sample below"* (localized; the English query term stays since the parser is English-grammar).
- The example pills are now clearly labelled **"Sample queries:"** / **"ಮಾದರಿ ಪ್ರಶ್ನೆಗಳು:"** / **"नमूना क्वेरी:"** (`over 10s · top 15 longest · shortest first · first 10`).

## 4. ✅ Testing plan: i18n is now a merge-gate for query parsing
`FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL §6.5` now mandates: the **moment** the query bar gains **semantic understanding** or accepts **Kannada/Hindi input** (or a conversational/NL builder), **i18n tests are required to merge** — a kn/hi query must parse to the correct selection and its parse-feedback must localize. (Today's parser is English-grammar-only — tracker A9.)

**103 web tests green** across all of it. The SPA remains fully **en + kn + hi**, offline-ready, with the success-criterion screencast as durable proof.

I have a complete, evidence-grounded picture. The Hindi catalog is fully populated and the LanguageSwitcher is wired with localStorage persistence and `?lang=` querystring negotiation. Here is the full report.

---

# Integration Surface Map: "GCS-bucket video → UI → downloaded reel" (+ Hindi repeat)

**Repos / branches read this session:** SPA `khelsutra/web/` (on `master`, up to date); engine `badminton-highlight-indexer/` on branch `feat/serving-m1-deployment-profile` (the "M1" branch; remote ref reported `gone`). Two design docs (`LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` DRAFT, `PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md` IN PROGRESS/M0-complete) were authored **this same session** against this exact source and are heavily `[verified]`-tagged — my findings corroborate them and add a few corrections (notably `/api/health` and `download_url` now **exist**, which those docs list as missing/pending).

---

## 1. Engine API inventory

All endpoints are in `backend/main.py` unless noted. Detector wiring is via the segmenter registry; the API itself is detector-agnostic.

| Endpoint | Method | File:line | What it does | Real vs stub |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/` | GET | `main.py:53` | Serves `backend/api/static/index.html` (the legacy static UI, **not** the React SPA) | n/a |
| `/api/config` | GET | `main.py:122` | Returns the whole typed `global_config` (SPA reads `stitching.min_shot_count`) | real |
| `/api/health` | GET | `main.py:126` | Liveness probe: `{status, sport, default_segmenter}`. **NOW EXISTS** (design docs say "expected but does not exist" — stale) | real |
| `/api/process` | POST → 202 | `main.py:254` | Submit a job. Fast-fails on bad path / missing file / unknown segmenter, then enqueues; returns `{job_id, status:"queued", poll}`. Slow work (incl. MD5 hashing + segmentation) runs on the worker thread | real pipeline |
| `/api/jobs/{job_id}` | GET | `main.py:288` | Job state `{status, progress, video_id, error, result, reports}`. `result.video_id` is how a finished job hands a `video_id` to the picker | real |
| `/api/query` | POST | `main.py:345` | List a video's rallies → `{play_segments:[...]}`. **The only rally-list path.** | real (reads DB) |
| `/api/compile` | POST | `main.py:356` | Stitch `segment_ids` → one MP4; applies the silent `min_shot_count` floor (`:386`); returns `{status, filepath, download_url}` | real |
| `/api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}` | GET | `main.py:321` | `FileResponse` streaming the compiled reel from `output_dir` (path-traversal-guarded). **This is the browser-reachable download endpoint** | real |
| `/api/upload/init` | POST | `uploads.py:64` | Start chunked upload → `{upload_id, max_part_mb, next_part}` | real |
| `/api/upload/{id}/part/{i}` | PUT | `uploads.py:89` | Append one sequential raw-bytes part (per-part + total caps) | real |
| `/api/upload/{id}/complete` | POST | `uploads.py:123` | Assemble → `{path, video_id, ...}` ready for `/api/process` | real |
| `/api/upload/{id}` | DELETE | `uploads.py:147` | Abort + delete partial | real |

**Absent (confirmed by the design docs, not re-grepped exhaustively):** `GET /api/capabilities`, `GET /api/videos` (only single-row `get_video(id)`), `GET /api/reels/{video_id}`, any source/clip/thumbnail streaming endpoint, any Dockerfile, any cost ledger.

**Worker (not an HTTP endpoint):** `python -m backend.worker {infer|train}` (`backend/worker/__main__.py`) is a **separate CLI dispatcher**, not reachable from the FastAPI loop. It is the only thing that speaks bucket URIs today (see Q2).

---

## 2. Bucket ingest — can the engine process a GCS video today?

**Yes, but only via the worker CLI — not via the HTTP API.**

- **GCS is a first-class, fully-implemented `StorageBackend`** (`backend/storage/gcs.py`): `fetch_to_local` downloads a blob (`:30-39`), `put` uploads (`:41-43`), plus `list`/`exists`/`stat`/`signed_url`. It lazy-imports `google-cloud-storage` (`:24`) and uses **Application Default Credentials** (`GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` SA-JSON or workload identity — never config; `gcs.py` module docstring `:1-6`).
- **URI routing:** `parse_uri` (`backend/storage/ref.py:48-68`) recognizes `gcs://bucket/key` and normalizes `gs://` → `gcs://` (`:60-61`). `__init__.py::fetch/put/list_uris` (`:56-77`) dispatch on the scheme. Config `StorageConfig` (`models.py:295-319`) supports `backend: Literal["local","s3","gcs","gdrive"]` (default `"local"`) with a per-backend `gcs: dict` settings slot (`:318`).
- **The worker `infer` path does the pull → pipeline → push** (`worker/__main__.py:cmd_infer`):
  - `:101` — `storage.fetch(args.video_uri, scratch/in.mp4)` downloads the GCS blob to local scratch.
  - `:105` — computes the `video_id` (MD5 of the pulled bytes).
  - `:130` — runs the configured segmenter (same registry as the API).
  - `:160-175` — serializes `outputs/<video_id>/{intervals.csv,report.json}` and **pushes them back** to `--out-uri` (which can itself be `gcs://`).

  CLI: `python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gcs://bucket/match.mp4 --out-uri gcs://bucket/out [--segmenter ...] [--set k=v]`.

**Critical gap for the CUJ:** the worker writes `intervals.csv` + `report.json` to a bucket — it does **NOT** write to the API's SQLite `play_segments` table, and it does **NOT** create a `videos` row that `/api/query` / `/api/compile` can read. So a worker `infer` run alone does **not** give the SPA a queryable `video_id`. Conversely, `POST /api/process` only accepts a **local** `video_path` (`ProcessRequest.video_path`, validated by `validate_input_path` + `os.path.exists` at `main.py:264-269`) — it has **no bucket-URI ingest**. There is no HTTP "ingest from `gcs://`" endpoint. This is exactly the "ingest-from-bucket wiring is **net-new**" gap the appliance doc flags (`LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.2 row "Bucket URI" = **design**, and the reserved "bucket-worker bridge" T14).

**Other backends:** `s3` (AWS/R2/DO-Spaces/MinIO via `endpoint_url`) is fully implemented (`s3.py`); `gdrive` (`gdrive.py`) reads a **locally-mounted** Google Drive as plain files — usable but not a remote API client.

---

## 3. Detectors / compute — what a real dry-run needs

Segmenters registered (`segmenters/__init__.py`, `list_available()`): `motion`, `gemini`, `yolo_hybrid`, `tracknet_hybrid`, `wasb_hybrid`, `fusion_hybrid`.

- **`config.json` default = `gemini`** (root `config.json` `indexing.default_segmenter: "gemini"`). (Note: the Pydantic *code* default is `yolo_hybrid` at `models.py:126`, but the shipped `config.json` overrides it to `gemini`.)
- **Gemini path (cloud, real):** `gemini.py:86-93` **requires `GEMINI_API_KEY`** in the environment and raises a clear error if absent. Runs on CPU (uploads downsized chunks to Google). **Needs: a Gemini API key + network. No GPU, no WSL.** This is the realistic CPU dry-run detector.
- **Native WASB GPU path:** `wasb_hybrid` → `TrajectoryHybridSegmenter._resolve_runner` (`trajectory_hybrid.py:65-86`). `detector_impl` ∈ `{auto=WSL, native=Linux-native GPU, stub=CPU mock}` (default `"auto"`, `models.py:133`; `RALLY_DETECTOR_IMPL` env overrides). **Needs a GPU host** (+ WSL for `auto`, or a native Linux GPU box for `native`). `trajectory_hybrid._build_native_runner` still **raises NotImplementedError** (`:57-63`) — only `wasb_hybrid` overrides it with a real `NativeWasbRunner` (`wasb_hybrid.py:40-46`). Native is **owner-byte-parity-gated, not production-verified** (per `LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §3, risk table).
- **`motion` (CPU, real but low-quality):** `motion.py` — pure OpenCV frame-differencing, **no key, no GPU**. BUT it **fabricates** `server`/`receiver` (`random.sample`), `shot_count`, and `events` (`motion.py:191-239`) — which the SPA is explicitly told never to surface (`types.ts:20-26`, `PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md` §3 "Fabricated — do NOT surface"). It produces real *segment boundaries* though.
- **`stub` (CPU mock):** `detectors/stub_runner.py` — deterministic synthetic trajectories, **no GPU/WSL/weights**. Combined with `--set indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true` it is the validated **secret-free, GPU-free** path (`worker/__main__.py:9-11`). Output is **not real detection** ("mock — not real detection" per the doc's honesty matrix).

**CPU-only paths that produce real-ish rallies:** `gemini` (real, needs key) and `motion` (real boundaries, fabricated metadata). `stub` is mock. **For a true "real rallies, no GPU" dry-run, Gemini + a key is the path.** A GPU is only required for native/WSL WASB.

---

## 4. Reel download to the local machine

**Fully working today.** Two-step, browser-reachable:

1. `POST /api/compile` writes the MP4 into `output_dir` and returns BOTH a server-local `filepath` **and** `download_url` (`main.py:410-411`): `download_url = f"/api/highlights/{quote(video_id)}/{quote(out_name)}"`. **The `download_url` field exists** (the "M1" addition — `PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md` P6 lists it as `☐`/TODO, but it is in the code now).
2. `GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}` (`main.py:321-342`) streams the file via `FileResponse` with the right media type — the browser-hittable endpoint.

**SPA side:** the SPA does **not** consume `download_url`. Its `CompileResponse` type (`types.ts:33-37`) only declares `status` + `filepath`; after compile it **hand-builds** the URL itself via `highlightUrl(videoId, filename)` (`client.ts:86-88`, called in `App.tsx:133`), then renders an inline `<video src>` preview + an `<a href download>` link (`CompileResult.tsx:48-61`). So download-to-local **works end to end already** for any video that's in the engine's DB — the only redundancy is the SPA ignoring the server-provided `download_url`.

---

## 5. The SPA gap — what's missing for this CUJ

The SPA is **strictly job-tethered and read-only on ingest**. Hard evidence: a grep of `web/src` for `process|upload|jobs|capabilities|videos|reels|health|download_url|job_id` returned **zero matches**. The SPA's entire API surface is `client.ts`: `listRallies` (`/query`), `compileReel` (`/compile`), `highlightUrl` (`/highlights`), `getConfig` (`/config`). That's it.

Concretely missing:

- **No ingest/process UI at all.** The SPA cannot point at a video (bucket or local), cannot call `/api/process`, cannot call the `/api/upload/*` chunked endpoints, cannot pick a segmenter. It assumes a video is **already processed**.
- **How it gets a `video_id`:** purely from the URL query string — `?video=<id>` (`App.tsx:48`: `new URLSearchParams(...).get("video")`). With no `?video=`, it shows hardcoded `DEMO_RALLIES` (`App.tsx:30-37`). There is **no in-UI path** to obtain a `video_id` — no job submission, no poll of `/api/jobs/{id}`, no `/api/videos` list.
- **No progress/poll UI** (no `/api/jobs` polling — that's appliance doc screen S2, unbuilt).
- **No history/reload survival** (no `/api/videos` / `/api/reels` — S4, unbuilt; the endpoints don't even exist).

What the SPA **does** do well (M0 complete): load `?video=<id>` → `POST /api/query` (default all-selected) → deterministic query/select/bulk → `POST /api/compile` → `<video>` preview + download, with `min_shot_count` warning from `/api/config`, structured `cid`-traced logs, and full i18n (en/hi/kn). So the **back half** of the CUJ (review → select → compile → download) is built; the **front half** (point at a bucket video → process → get a `video_id`) is entirely missing from the UI.

---

## 6. Cheapest path to a dry run

The minimum to demonstrate "GCS video → UI → downloaded reel" **without building in-UI ingest** is to pre-seed the engine DB out-of-band, then drive the existing SPA back-half against it. The blocker is that **neither** the worker `infer` (writes CSV to bucket, not the DB) **nor** `/api/process` (local path only) bridges "GCS video → a queryable `video_id` in the API's SQLite". So the cheap path is:

**Steps (which already work vs must be run/built):**

1. **Get the bucket video onto the engine host as a local file** — `python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gcs://bucket/match.mp4 --out-uri gcs://bucket/out --set indexing.skip_ai_handoff=true` *fetches* it to scratch, OR simpler: `gsutil cp gcs://bucket/match.mp4 ./incoming/` (the `gcs.py` fetch already proves GCS read works). — *fetch works; trivial to run.*
2. **Process it through the HTTP API so it lands in the API's DB** — `POST /api/process {video_path: "<abs local path>", segmenter: "gemini"}` (needs `GEMINI_API_KEY`) → poll `GET /api/jobs/{job_id}` until `result.video_id` appears. This populates `videos` + `play_segments` that `/api/query` and `/api/compile` read. — *endpoint works; just needs a key + a real video.* (Or `segmenter: "motion"` for a key-free real-boundaries run, accepting fabricated-metadata caveats.)
3. **Open the SPA pointed at that video** — `https://<host>/?video=<video_id>` (dev: `npm run dev`, Vite proxies `/api` → `:8000`). The SPA auto-loads rallies via `POST /api/query`. — *works today.*
4. **Select → Build → download** — pick rallies, click Build → `POST /api/compile` → `<video>` preview + `<a download>` saves the MP4 locally. — *works today.*
5. **Repeat in Hindi** — click the हिन्दी button in `LanguageSwitcher` (or append `?lang=hi`, or set localStorage), re-run select→compile→download. — *works today* (Hindi catalog fully populated, `i18n/index.ts` + `locales/hi/common.json`).

**So the only genuinely missing glue is step 2's "point at GCS" being a CLI/manual step, not in the UI.** If you accept a 1-command pre-process to mint the `video_id`, the dry run needs **zero new code** — only a running engine with a Gemini key, the GCS video copied/fetched local, and the SPA opened with `?video=<id>`.

**The alternative (full in-UI ingest)** would require building appliance-doc screens S1/S2 + the **net-new bucket-ingest HTTP endpoint** (T12/T14) — much more work and explicitly out of the current MVP scope.

---

## 7. Dependencies / unknowns to decide or stand up

- **Engine host & OS-runnability:** The engine binds `127.0.0.1:8000` (`main.py:655`) and needs `ffmpeg` + OpenCV. It's pure-Python FastAPI and *should* run on Windows for the **Gemini/motion** CPU paths (no WSL needed). **WSL/Linux-GPU is required only for `wasb` native/auto detection.** The validated GPU-free e2e is documented on the **CPU DigitalOcean droplet** (`168.144.126.176`, blr1, CPU-only). Decide: run the dry-run engine on **local Windows** (Gemini-CPU, simplest), the **CPU droplet**, or a **GPU host** (only if you want real WASB). For the bucket dry-run, local Windows + Gemini is the fewest moving parts.
- **Compute choice:** Gemini-CPU (real, needs key, cloud upload — consent copy) vs `motion` (key-free, fabricated metadata hidden) vs GPU/WASB (owner-byte-parity-gated, not production-verified). For "real-ish rallies, cheapest" → **Gemini + key**.
- **Which bucket video:** must pass the engine's validators (`config.json` validation: ≥1280×720, ≥29.9 fps, blur/scene-cut/court-relevance checks) or `/api/process` returns `status:"failed"`. Pick a clean ≥720p badminton clip.
- **GCS auth:** `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS` → SA-JSON (or `gcloud` ADC). Never in config (`gcs.py` docstring). Only needed if you use the worker fetch rather than `gsutil`.
- **CORS / SPA→engine connection:** engine sets CORS `allow_origins=["*"]`, `allow_credentials=False` (`main.py:41-47`) — so cross-origin browser calls are permitted. In dev, Vite **proxies** `/api` → `localhost:8000` (`vite.config.ts`), so it's same-origin and CORS is moot. In prod the appliance design is a single reverse-proxy origin (the gate, not the app, is the boundary). For a quick dry-run, `npm run dev` against a local engine needs no CORS/auth work.
- **Edge auth:** none in the engine by design (`LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` §4.4 — Caddy basicauth or cloudflared+Access). Not required for a localhost dry-run; **required** before any non-localhost exposure (and **rotate the chat-shared `GEMINI_API_KEY`**).
- **The DB-bridge unknown (the real blocker):** there is **no supported in-API way to ingest a `gcs://` URI into a queryable `video_id`**. For the dry-run this is sidestepped by the local-copy + `/api/process` pre-step (Q6); productizing "bucket video from the UI" needs the net-new bucket-ingest endpoint (T12/T14) — an owner-gated decision.

### 628. assistant (2026-06-21T07:45:13.232Z)

The agent gave a precise, evidence-grounded map. Here's the key picture:

**The back half already works** (M0): the SPA does load `?video=<id>` → `/api/query` → select → `/api/compile` → **download the reel locally** (`/api/highlights/{id}/{name}` streams it; the engine even returns a `download_url` now). And it's fully en/kn/hi.

**The front half is the gap:** the SPA has **no in-UI ingest** — it can't point at a bucket video or call `/api/process`. And critically, **there's no supported "GCS URI → queryable `video_id`" path in the HTTP API**: `/api/process` only takes a *local* path, while the worker CLI processes `gcs://` videos but writes to the bucket, not the API's DB.

So there are two ways to hit your goal. Let me set this as a tracked goal with the runbook, then get your steer on scope + compute (those genuinely need your call — they involve your Gemini key, a video, and where to run the engine).

### 630. assistant (2026-06-21T07:45:52.875Z)

# Dry-run CUJ — UI drives the tool on a bucket video → reel downloaded local (multilingual)

> **Status:** `DRAFT` — owner-gated · **Owner:** Avi Dullu · **Created:** 2026-06-21 · **Goal for this session.**
> **Umbrella:** a milestone of `docs/LOCAL_APPLIANCE_ARCHITECTURE.md` (the appliance) + `docs/PER_RALLY_SELECTION.md` (the shipped SPA back-half).
> **Honesty note:** grounded in a file:line integration-surface map (this session); claims tagged `[verified]` / `[gap]`.

## 0. The goal (owner's words)
Operate the **web UI** → point at a **video on the GCS bucket** → the engine (tool) processes it → **review/select rallies in the UI** → **compile** → **download the reel to the local machine**. Then **repeat with the UI in Hindi** to prove the multilingual workflow.

## 1. What already works vs the gap `[verified]`
**✅ Back half (shipped — PER_RALLY_SELECTION M0):** the SPA loads `?video=<id>` → `POST /api/query` (rallies, default all-selected) → deterministic query/select → `POST /api/compile` → `<video>` preview + **`<a download>` saves the MP4 locally**. Reel download-to-local **works today** (`GET /api/highlights/{video_id}/{filename}` streams it; `/api/compile` also returns `download_url`). Fully **en/kn/hi** (the switcher / `?lang=`).

**⛔ Front half (the gap):**
- The SPA has **no ingest/process UI** — it can't point at a video (bucket or local), can't call `/api/process`, can't poll `/api/jobs`. It only gets a `video_id` from `?video=` in the URL.
- **No HTTP bucket-ingest:** `POST /api/process` accepts only a **local** `video_path`. The worker CLI (`python -m backend.worker infer --video-uri gcs://…`) *can* fetch+process a bucket video (GCS is a real `StorageBackend`), **but it writes `intervals.csv`/`report.json` to the bucket — NOT to the API's SQLite `videos`/`play_segments`**. So a worker run alone does not give the SPA a queryable `video_id`. **Bridging "gcs:// → a queryable video_id in the API DB" is net-new** (appliance T12/T14).

## 2. Two paths to the goal
**Path A — Cheapest dry run NOW (~zero new code).** Pre-process the bucket video out-of-band to mint a `video_id` in the engine DB, then drive the existing SPA back-half. Proves the **multilingual UI-driven workflow** end-to-end. The only non-UI step is "point at the bucket" (a one-line CLI pre-process).

**Path B — Full "point at a bucket video IN the UI" (bigger, multi-session).** Build the in-UI ingest: a net-new **HTTP bucket-ingest endpoint** (`gcs://` → process → DB `video_id`) + the SPA **ingest + progress** screens (appliance S1/S2). The proper appliance front-half.

## 3. Path A runbook (the cheapest dry run) `[verified-steps]`
1. **Engine host + deps:** run the engine FastAPI (`python -m backend.main --server`, binds `127.0.0.1:8000`) on a box with `ffmpeg` + OpenCV. Gemini/motion paths are **CPU-only, no WSL** → local Windows works; the CPU droplet works too. (GPU/WSL only needed for native WASB.)
2. **Get the bucket video local + into the API DB:**
   `gsutil cp gs://<bucket>/<match>.mp4 ./incoming/` (or the worker fetch), then `POST /api/process {"video_path":"<abs local path>","segmenter":"gemini"}` → poll `GET /api/jobs/{job_id}` until `result.video_id`. *(`gemini` needs `GEMINI_API_KEY`; `motion` is key-free but hides fabricated metadata.)*
3. **Open the SPA against the engine:** `npm run dev` (Vite proxies `/api` → `:8000`, same-origin) → `http://localhost:5173/?video=<video_id>` → rallies auto-load.
4. **Select → Build → download:** pick rallies → Build → `<video>` preview → click the download link → MP4 saved local.
5. **Repeat in Hindi:** click **हिन्दी** (or `?lang=hi`) → redo select→compile→download → confirm the whole flow works with the UI in Hindi.

**Small unblocked SPA polish that makes Path A more "via the UI"** (independent of A/B, useful in both): a **"Load a video by ID"** input (paste the `video_id` → load, instead of editing the URL) + consume the engine's `download_url` (instead of hand-building it). One tight PR.

## 4. Open decisions — OWNER (these gate the dry run)
1. **Scope:** Path A (cheapest dry run now) or Path B (build the full in-UI bucket ingest)? *Recommend A first* (proves the multilingual workflow fast), then B as the appliance build.
2. **Compute / detector:** **Gemini** (real rallies; needs your `GEMINI_API_KEY`; uploads downsized chunks to Google = paid + cloud) · **motion** (key-free, runs anywhere, but lower-quality boundaries + fabricated server/shot metadata stays hidden) · **GPU WASB** (best quality, but needs a GPU host + is owner-parity-gated, not production-verified). *Recommend Gemini for "real-ish, cheapest" — pending your key + OK (paid + uploads footage).*
3. **Engine host:** local Windows (simplest, Gemini/motion) · the CPU droplet `168.144.126.176` (shared testbed — careful) · a GPU VM (only for WASB).
4. **Which bucket video:** e.g. `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/mahadevpura_smoke_180s.mp4` (already staged, known-good) or another clean ≥720p clip (must pass the engine validators).

## 5. Honest caveats
- **Gemini = paid + uploads footage to Google** (an external/paid action — needs your explicit OK + a clean clip). **Rotate the chat-shared `GEMINI_API_KEY`.**
- **motion** fabricates `server`/`receiver`/`shot_count` (the SPA correctly hides them) — boundaries are real but metadata isn't.
- **GPU/native WASB** is partially wired + owner-byte-parity-gated → **not production-verified**.
- Path A's "point at the bucket" is a **CLI pre-step**, not in-UI — full in-UI ingest is Path B.
- Edge auth: none in the engine (localhost dry-run is fine; required before any non-localhost exposure).

## 6. Definition of done (the dry run)
- [ ] A bucket video → (engine processes) → the **SPA loads its rallies** → select → compile → **reel downloaded to the local machine**, all driven from the UI (Path A allows a one-line CLI pre-process).
- [ ] The **same flow works with the UI in Hindi** (and Kannada) — multilingual workflow confirmed.
- [ ] *(Path B, if chosen)* the bucket video is pointed-at **from the UI** (no CLI pre-step).

1	"""Async job worker — drain/wake loop (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16, EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3).
2	
3	Extracted verbatim from backend.main to shrink the orchestrator; behavior is unchanged.
4	The worker DRAINS the jobs table via `claim_due_job` and honours WAIT_AND_RESUME: a job
5	that raises `JobWaiting` is parked (status 'waiting' + `next_poll_at` + progress) and
6	re-claimed when due — with no central scheduler (the DB is the clock).
7	
8	The monkeypatch contract these functions rely on (and that tests depend on):
9	every cross-function seam — `db_instance`, `global_config`, `_execute_processing`,
10	`_arm_wake_timer`, `_get_executor`, `_MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC` — is resolved THROUGH the
11	`backend.main` module object at call-time (`import backend.main as _main`), never as a
12	bare local. `backend.main` re-exports these names, so `monkeypatch.setattr(backend.main,
13	...)` still intercepts the inter-function calls (e.g. a parked job arms the *patched*
14	`_arm_wake_timer`, so no real daemon timer leaks in tests). Importing `backend.main` lazily
15	inside the functions also avoids the circular import at module load (main.py imports this
16	module; this module must not import main at import time).
17	"""
18	import logging
19	import threading
20	import traceback
21	from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
22	from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
23	
24	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
25	
26	# Async job executor (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). max_workers=1 on purpose: a single
27	# self-hosted box serializes GPU work, and the Gemini provider is rate-fragile under
28	# parallel uploads (2026-06-10 ops learning: serial uploads + backoff). The segmenters
29	# already parallelize their AI handoff internally via their own pools.
30	_job_executor: Optional[ThreadPoolExecutor] = None
31	
32	
33	def _get_executor() -> ThreadPoolExecutor:
34	    """Lazy module-global executor; tests monkeypatch this for inline execution."""
35	    global _job_executor
36	    if _job_executor is None:
37	        _job_executor = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1, thread_name_prefix="jobworker")
38	    return _job_executor
39	
40	
41	class JobWaiting(Exception):
42	    """Raised inside the pipeline to SELF-SCHEDULE a job (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3,
43	    WAIT_AND_RESUME). It means "I can't finish now — park me and resume in `delay_sec`",
44	    NOT a failure. The worker persists `next_poll_at` + `progress` (via `set_job_waiting`),
45	    releases the thread, and re-claims the job when due (`claim_due_job`). No work to date
46	    is the segmenter's `_execute_processing`, run UNDER the destroy-after-confirm contract.
47	
48	    The wiring is additive: the production segmenter path never raises this today (zero
49	    behaviour change), so a job runs exactly as before. The hook is what a later slice
50	    (resumable AI handoff) raises once a long op exhausts its in-process wait budget.
51	    """
52	
53	    def __init__(self, delay_sec: float, progress: Optional[str] = None) -> None:
54	        super().__init__(f"job parked for {delay_sec:.0f}s")
55	        self.delay_sec = max(0.0, float(delay_sec))
56	        self.progress = progress
57	
58	
59	def _job_to_request(job: Dict[str, Any]):
60	    """Reconstruct the submit-time ProcessRequest from a claimed job row, so a job
61	    re-claimed by `claim_due_job` (queued OR resumed-from-waiting) runs identically to
62	    the original submit. The row stores exactly the ProcessRequest fields."""
63	    import backend.main as _main
64	    return _main.ProcessRequest(
65	        video_path=job["video_path"],
66	        player_pool=job.get("player_pool"),
67	        max_frames=job.get("max_frames"),
68	        segmenter=job.get("segmenter"),
69	    )
70	
71	
72	def _run_claimed_job(job: Dict[str, Any]) -> None:
73	    """Run ONE job already claimed (`status='running'`) by `claim_due_job`, recording its
74	    terminal state — OR parking it for resume on `JobWaiting` (WAIT_AND_RESUME).
75	
76	    Job `status` is the EXECUTION state (queued|waiting|running|done|failed): 'done' means
77	    the worker finished — the pipeline's own verdict (validation failure etc.) lives in
78	    result["status"]; 'failed' means the worker itself crashed/raised; 'waiting' means the
79	    job self-scheduled and will be re-claimed when `next_poll_at` is due.
80	    """
81	    import backend.main as _main
82	    db_instance = _main.db_instance
83	    job_id = job["id"]
84	    req = _main._job_to_request(job)
85	    try:
86	        result = _main._execute_processing(
87	            req, progress=lambda stage: db_instance.set_job_progress(job_id, stage))
88	        if result.get("video_id"):
89	            db_instance.set_job_video_id(job_id, result["video_id"])
90	        db_instance.mark_job_done(job_id, result)
91	    except _main.JobWaiting as w:
92	        # Cooperative re-enqueue: persist progress + next_poll_at, release the worker.
93	        logger.info(f"Job {job_id} parked for {w.delay_sec:.0f}s (resume when due).")
94	        db_instance.set_job_waiting(job_id, w.delay_sec, progress=w.progress)
95	    except Exception as e:
96	        logger.error(f"Job {job_id} crashed: {e}\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
97	        db_instance.mark_job_failed(job_id, f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}")
98	
99	
100	def _drain_due_jobs() -> int:
101	    """One worker tick: claim and run every currently-runnable job (queued, or waiting whose
102	    `next_poll_at` is due), earliest-due first, until the table has nothing runnable.
103	
104	    Each parked job (`JobWaiting` → status 'waiting') drops out of this drain and is picked
105	    up by a FUTURE drain once due; we schedule that follow-up drain via `_schedule_drain` so
106	    a single-worker box keeps making progress with no central timer (the DB is the clock).
107	    Returns the number of jobs that reached a terminal/parked state this tick.
108	    """
109	    import backend.main as _main
110	    ran = 0
111	    while True:
112	        job = _main.db_instance.claim_due_job()
113	        if job is None:
114	            break
115	        ran += 1
116	        _main._run_claimed_job(job)
117	    # If anything is parked-but-not-yet-due, arrange a wake-up so it resumes without a new
118	    # submit. On the single-box executor this is a delayed re-drain; multi-worker just relies
119	    # on the next submit/drain. Best-effort — never raises into the worker.
120	    _main._schedule_next_drain_if_waiting()
121	    return ran
122	
123	
124	def _schedule_next_drain_if_waiting() -> None:
125	    """If any job is parked in 'waiting', arm a re-drain for when the soonest one is due, so a
126	    self-scheduled job resumes with no further client submit. The drain itself re-checks
127	    due-ness, so an early wake is harmless and a clamped wake for a far-future job is fine."""
128	    import backend.main as _main
129	    try:
130	        delay = _main.db_instance.seconds_until_next_due()
131	    except Exception as e:  # pragma: no cover - defensive; never wedge the worker
132	        logger.error(f"Could not compute next-due delay: {e}")
133	        return
134	    if delay is None:
135	        return  # nothing waiting
136	    # Clamp so a far-future job doesn't pin a thread forever; the drain re-checks due-ness.
137	    _main._arm_wake_timer(max(0.0, min(delay, _main._MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC)))
138	
139	
140	def _arm_wake_timer(delay_sec: float) -> None:
141	    """Arm a single daemon timer that re-submits a drain after ``delay_sec`` (no central
142	    scheduler — the DB is the clock). Isolated as the sole timer-creating seam so tests can
143	    monkeypatch it to a no-op and assert the *scheduling decision* without real waiting."""
144	    import backend.main as _main
145	    timer = threading.Timer(delay_sec, lambda: _main._get_executor().submit(_main._drain_due_jobs))
146	    timer.daemon = True
147	    timer.start()
148	
149	
150	#: A parked job further out than this is re-checked by a capped wake rather than one long
151	#: sleeping timer — keeps the wake cadence bounded without a central scheduler.
152	_MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC = 60.0
153

def process_video(req: ProcessRequest):
    """Submit a processing job (202 + job_id). Poll GET /api/jobs/{job_id} for state.

    Fast-fail checks (bad path, missing file, unknown segmenter) happen here so the
    client gets an immediate 4xx; everything slow — including MD5 hashing of the file —
    runs on the worker (DEFERRED_HARDENING_PLAN #16). The row is created 'queued'; the
    worker drains it via `claim_due_job` (EXECUTION_POLICY.md P3 self-scheduling loop).
    """
    allowed_root = getattr(getattr(global_config, "security", None), "allowed_video_root", None)
    try:
        validate_input_path(req.video_path, allowed_root=allowed_root)
    except ValueError as e:
        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail=str(e)) from e
    if not os.path.exists(req.video_path):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"Video file not found at '{req.video_path}'")
    if req.segmenter and not segmenter_registry.get(req.segmenter):
        available = list(segmenter_registry.list_available().keys())
        raise HTTPException(
            status_code=400,
            detail=f"Segmenter '{req.segmenter}' not found. Available segmenters: {available}"
        )

    job_id = uuid.uuid4().hex
    if not db_instance.create_job(job_id, req.video_path, req.segmenter,
                                  req.player_pool, req.max_frames):
        raise HTTPException(status_code=500, detail="Failed to persist job record.")
    _get_executor().submit(_drain_due_jobs)
    return JSONResponse(
        status_code=202,
        content={"job_id": job_id, "status": "queued", "poll": f"/api/jobs/{job_id}"},
    )

### 693. assistant (2026-06-21T08:04:10.867Z)

Found it — the likely root cause. Running the engine via **`python -m backend.main`** loads `main.py` as `__main__` (where `main()` runs and sets `db_instance`), but `job_worker` does `import backend.main as _main` — which loads a **second, separate** `backend.main` module where `main()` never runs, so the drain's `_main.db_instance` points at an unconfigured DB and never sees the queued job. The tests don't catch it (they monkeypatch the executor to run inline). Let me confirm the `global` declarations + module-level init.

### 695. assistant (2026-06-21T08:05:18.273Z)

**Confirmed — this is a real, latent engine bug.** Running via `python -m backend.main` (the documented invocation) executes `main()` in `__main__` (setting `__main__.db_instance`), but `job_worker`/`uploads` resolve shared state via `import backend.main` — a **separate** module object where `main()` never ran, so `db_instance` is `None` there. The drain crashes silently on `None.claim_due_job()` → jobs hang in `queued` forever. The test suite misses it because it monkeypatches the executor to run inline, and the M0 e2e used a stub engine.

Let me unblock the dry run by running the engine through the **single module** (`from backend import main; main.main()`), then I'll make the proper fix a merged engine PR.

33	    assert m._MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC == 60.0

37	    """THE HAZARD PIN: a drain that finds a not-yet-due 'waiting' job must arm the
38	    wake-timer THROUGH `backend.main._arm_wake_timer`, so patching it on
39	    `backend.main` intercepts and no live daemon timer is created. Patch
40	    `m._arm_wake_timer` (the documented seam), run a real drain, and assert it was
41	    the patched recorder that fired — not a bare local that would leak a timer."""
42	    from backend.infrastructure.database import Database
43

45	    monkeypatch.setattr(m, "db_instance", db)
46	    armed: list = []

50	    db.set_job_waiting("j1", delay_sec=3600, progress="parked")

52	    ran = m._drain_due_jobs()
53	
54	    assert ran == 0  # not due -> not claimed
55	    assert db.get_job("j1")["status"] == "waiting"
56	    # The patched seam fired (clamped to the cap) — proves the indirection holds.
57	    assert armed and 0.0 < armed[-1] <= m._MAX_DRAIN_WAKE_SEC

66	    class _RecordingExecutor:

82	            self.fn_result = self.fn()  # fire immediately, no real wait
83

85	        def fn(self):
86	            return captured["fn"]

### 755. assistant (2026-06-21T08:18:16.346Z)

"""Regression guard for the `python -m backend.main` double-module job-hang.

`python -m backend.main` runs main.py as `__main__`, so `main()` configures `__main__`'s globals
(`db_instance`/`global_config`). But `job_worker`/`uploads` resolve shared state via
`import backend.main` — a SEPARATE module object whose `db_instance` stays `None`. The async-job
drain then crashes silently on `None.claim_due_job()` and every submitted job hangs in `queued`
forever. The in-process tests (TestClient + monkeypatched `db_instance` + an inline executor)
cannot see this — only the real subprocess does. This spawns the DOCUMENTED invocation and asserts
a submitted job actually LEAVES `queued`.
"""
import json
import os
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

import pytest

REPO_ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))


def _free_port() -> int:
    s = socket.socket()
    s.bind(("127.0.0.1", 0))
    port = int(s.getsockname()[1])
    s.close()
    return port


def _get(url: str, timeout: float = 5):
    with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=timeout) as r:
        return json.loads(r.read().decode())


def _post(url: str, payload: dict, timeout: float = 15):
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        url, data=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers={"content-type": "application/json"})
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as r:
        return json.loads(r.read().decode())


def test_python_m_backend_main_drains_submitted_jobs(tmp_path):
    """Spawning the engine the documented way (`python -m backend.main --server`) must actually
    DRAIN a submitted job — it must leave `queued`, never hang. (A dummy file fails fast; the
    verdict is irrelevant — what matters is that the worker claimed and ran it.)"""
    port = _free_port()
    out_dir = tmp_path / "out"
    video = tmp_path / "dummy.mp4"
    video.write_bytes(b"not-a-real-video")  # exists -> the drain claims it; processing fails fast
    log = open(tmp_path / "server.log", "wb")

    proc = subprocess.Popen(
        [sys.executable, "-m", "backend.main", "--server", "--port", str(port),
         "--output-dir", str(out_dir)],
        cwd=REPO_ROOT, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
    )
    base = f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}"
    try:
        deadline = time.time() + 90  # cold import of torch/cv2 can be slow on CI
        while time.time() < deadline:
            if proc.poll() is not None:
                pytest.fail(f"server exited early (code {proc.returncode}); see {tmp_path/'server.log'}")
            try:
                if _get(f"{base}/api/health").get("status") == "ok":
                    break
            except (urllib.error.URLError, ConnectionError, OSError):
                time.sleep(0.5)
        else:
            pytest.fail("server never became healthy")

        job_id = _post(f"{base}/api/process",
                       {"video_path": str(video), "segmenter": "motion"})["job_id"]

        status = "queued"
        deadline = time.time() + 45
        while time.time() < deadline:
            status = _get(f"{base}/api/jobs/{job_id}")["status"]
            if status != "queued":
                break
            time.sleep(1)

        assert status != "queued", (
            "job stayed 'queued' — the async drain never ran under `python -m backend.main` "
            "(the __main__/backend.main double-module entrypoint bug regressed)."
        )
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        try:
            proc.wait(timeout=10)
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            proc.kill()
        log.close()

## ✅ Dry-run CUJ — Path A (local), English AND Hindi: **done**

I wired the SPA to a real engine and drove the whole flow from the UI:

1. **Bucket video** `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/videos/mahadevpura_smoke_180s.mp4` → pulled local → `POST /api/process {gemini}`.
2. **Engine processed it via Gemini** → **12 rallies** (`video_id 05e0e577…`).
3. **SPA loaded the 12 real rallies** (`?video=05e0e577…`, "Loaded 12 rallies from this session").
4. **Build → compile → reel (1920×1080, 76.5s) downloaded to your local machine:** `~/Downloads/khelsutra-dryrun/highlights_local_en.mp4` (50.9 MB).
5. **Repeated with the UI in Hindi** — the screenshot earlier shows it: Hindi page + 12 real rally cards + "रील बनाएँ" + the compiled reel playing ("आपकी रील तैयार है") + "highlights.mp4 डाउनलोड करें" → `highlights_local_hi.mp4` downloaded. **Multilingual workflow confirmed.**

Tracked + recorded in [`docs/DRY_RUN_CUJ.md`](docs/DRY_RUN_CUJ.md) (#53/#54).

## ▶▶ The run surfaced a **critical engine bug** — found, fixed, **merged** (engine [#281](https://github.com/Khelsutra/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/281))
Running the engine the **documented** way (`python -m backend.main --server`) made **every `/api/process` job hang in `queued` forever**. Root cause: `python -m` runs `main.py` as `__main__` (sets `__main__.db_instance`), but `job_worker`/`uploads` read `import backend.main` — a **separate** module whose `db_instance` is `None` → the async-job drain crashes silently. It was latent because the unit tests monkeypatch the executor inline and the UI had only been tested against a *stub* engine — the real async path was never run end-to-end until now.
- **Fix:** dispatch `__main__` → `backend.main`. **+ a real-subprocess regression test** that **fails-without / passes-with**. Full engine suite green (2m23s); ruff + mypy clean. **Merged.**

## ✅ Flyer screenshots (your low-pri aesthetic ask) — [#52](https://github.com/avidullu/khelsutra/pull/52), merged
Each flyer now shows the tool rendered in its language (Kannada UI on the Kannada flyer, Hindi on the Hindi one), screen-only so the printed A4 stays clean.

## ⚠ Option 3 (the droplet) — **not yet run; needs your steer**
The droplet (`168.144.126.176`) is reachable (Linux, Python 3.12, ffmpeg) but is **un-provisioned** — there's no engine checkout there anymore (`~/rally` is gone), and it's a **2-core / 3.8 GB** box. So an option-3 run means a **from-scratch engine install** (torch + opencv + the engine stack) on a small shared box — heavy, and tight on RAM. The fix I just merged applies there identically.

# 📋 Session Summary — KhelSutra: friendly, multilingual, and proven end-to-end

**The headline:** the product went from English-only to **fully trilingual (en · ಕನ್ನಡ · हिन्दी)**, and we proved the whole loop live — **a GCS-bucket video → the engine → a highlight reel downloaded locally, driven entirely from the UI, in English *and* Hindi** — which surfaced and fixed a **critical latent engine bug**. **~16 PRs merged**, all CI-green.

### 🌏 1. Made the product multilingual (the `FRIENDLY_AND_MULTILINGUAL` project)
Isolated into one tracked project, then built it slice by slice:
| | What |
|---|---|
| **Foundation** | react-i18next + ICU + browser-language detector; negotiation order persisted→`?lang`→browser→`en`; a **language switcher** |
| **Full extraction** | every SPA string → `en` catalog + **key-parity & no-bare-strings CI** |
| **Fonts** | self-hosted **OFL Noto** Kannada/Devanagari (bundled woff2, `unicode-range`-scoped) |
| **Catalogs** | full **Kannada** + **Hindi** (the "add-a-language = add-a-catalog" extensibility proof) |
| **Rigor** | a **§6.5 negotiation test matrix** (a kn/hi *browser* auto-gets its language) + `<html lang>` sync + an **offline/air-gap CI guard** + a localized **sample-query prompt** |
| **Proof** | a **Playwright screencast**: a Kannada-default browser auto-renders Kannada → switch hi → en |

### 🎬 2. Drove the real CUJ end-to-end (the dry run)
Wired the SPA to a live engine and ran it: bucket video → **Gemini** → **12 rallies** → UI loaded them → Build → **reel (1080p, 76.5s) downloaded locally** — then **repeated the whole thing with the UI in Hindi**.

### 🐛 3. Found + fixed a critical engine bug (merged)
The async-job server **hung every job in `queued`** when run the documented way (`python -m backend.main`) — a `__main__`-vs-`backend.main` double-module split left the job-drain's `db_instance` `None`. Latent for ages (tests monkeypatch the executor; the UI was only tested vs a stub). **Fix + a real-subprocess regression test** (fails-without / passes-with) → merged.

### 🪧 4. Polish
Linked the en/kn/hi academy flyers from the footer + nav + own-model, and put a **localized UI screenshot on each flyer** (verified: prints in exactly 1 A4 page).

### 📦 PR ledger (all merged)
`khelsutra`: **#41–#54** (project doc + pointers, B1 scaffold, B2 extract, B4 fonts, B5 kn, B6 hi, tracker, offline guard, sample prompt, flyer links ×2, flyer screenshots, dry-run doc ×2).
`engine`: **#275** (design-doc pointers) · **#281** (the double-module fix).

- `~/Downloads/khelsutra-dryrun/highlights_local_{en,hi}.mp4` (the downloaded reels)
- `gs://khelsutra-rally-corpus/promo/khelsutra-i18n-screencast.mp4` (the i18n screencast)
